# In-House ERP: Micro-Checklist & Build Tracker

The build tracker, arranged by ERP module: **24 modules**, each split into sub-modules that hold the items. The skeleton is permanent - a module or sub-module with nothing left still shows, marked *Nothing open.* Built items move to **[the archive](archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md)**, which has the same skeleton, so a module's history sits in the same place in both files.

- **Items.** `[ ]` open, `[x]` built and verified (build/vet/test, plus a live check where the item calls for it). Each item ends with `*Status: ...*` - Not started · Building · Built, not verified · Done · Needs decision · Blocked - set when the item is recorded and updated once more when the work finishes or blocks.
- **Ids.** An item keeps its id for life; code comments cite them (e.g. `47.11.4`). The number before the first dot is the ask the item came from - these were called stages until 2026-11-10. The write-ups of the old stage sections are in the archive's **[Background notes](archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md#background-notes)**, filed by module; an ask since then has its run header in the **Asks log**.
- **Every ask is an item (user rule, 2026-10-18).** Whatever the user asks - a large piece of work or a one-line fix - is recorded before building starts: a run header at the end of the **Asks log** (date, the user's words, the item ids, a status) and each item in the sub-module it changes. A new ask takes the next free number - `pwsh docs/update-checklist-map.ps1` prints it - and numbers its items N.1, N.2, ...; never open a `## Stage` section. Nothing the user said may exist only in chat. New asks add no Background notes: context goes in the run header, the narrative once in `project_ledger.md`.
- **Where an item goes.** The sub-module a user would look under for that screen or behaviour: a PO screen fix is *PROC › Purchase orders*, not *UI*; *UI* holds only what every screen shares (menus, window fit, inputs, wording). One item, one place. A new sub-module only when nothing fits, added to both files in the same position; a new module only on the user's say-so.
- **Counts.** After editing either file run `pwsh docs/update-checklist-map.ps1`: it refreshes the Module map below and the *Nothing open.* markers; `-Check` reports drift without writing.
- **Archiving.** An archive pass moves `[x]` items, with their sub-items, to the same sub-module in the archive. An item with an open sub-item, an open decision or an unverified status stays here.

> **Legend for historical notes below**: some items were marked `[x]` early and later found to be missing real implementation — those carry a "re-opened"/"correction" note explaining the gap and the fix. This is expected project history, not something to clean up.

### PIM › Pricing, MRP & market prices

- [ ] **34.4** JSON-endpoint harvester, **stdlib only**. This is feasible precisely because of the reference doc's headline finding: **Myntra ships its whole search result set as an embedded `window.__myx` JSON blob** (fallback `window.__m_initial_state`), so `net/http` + `regexp` + `encoding/json` are sufficient — **no browser and no HTML parser**. Adopt the retired project's two-phase shape: harvest raw responses to disk under a bounded worker pool, skip any file already present and non-trivial (free resumability *and* cache), then parse offline in a second pass so a parser bug costs no re-fetch and no re-ban. **[needs user decision — gated on 34.6]** Ask 168 (erp-0b, 2026-11-13): built for Myntra only - `platform.market_harvest_myntra` (off), max pages and interval settings; robots.txt honoured, no redirects followed, generic User-Agent, data per client; PIM > Reports > Market prices runs it (admin). PIM-9340..9343. Tests on saved pages. Not run against myntra.com: needs the owner's go-ahead. *Status: Blocked - owner checklist §6 (built, not verified live)*

### WMS › Bins, LPNs & storage

- [x] **230.3** A bin cannot be found by its code. Every link picker searches `q_fields=@identity` (127.7), and a Bin's identity is `id`/`code`/`name` - not `bin_code`, the code people know and the one the Bin picker shows and stores. Typing "SOP-A1-..." in Putaway's Bin Code box finds nothing; only the generated id (BI00001) matches. LPN (`lpn_code`) and Hold (`hold_code`) have the same gap. Found while re-recording the SOP videos (ask 230). Fix once in `SearchIdentityFields`: a doctype's own `<doctype>_code` field is identity. Built: `ownCodeField` adds `<doctype>_code` to @identity (only Bin, LPN and Hold have one). Verified: new `TestSearchIdentityOwnCode` plus `TestSearchIdentityScope` pass (`internal/server`, on a fully migrated DB), `go vet ./engines/` clean; the Bin search that returned nothing now finds both SOP-A1 bins, and the SOP putaway clip picks its bin by code. Guides already said "enter the Bin Code" - no change. *Status: Done*

So, There will be two phone number from OMS."* The order keeps the phone as received and a cleaned one (India: 10 digits; abroad: + country code); the bill and the customer use the clean one. The order page shows **Sent as**. Verified: `TestOrderKeepsPhoneAsReceived` (+91 spaced/dashed, trunk 0, 91-prefixed, +44; print shows +447700900123), `TestSearchSalesHistory`, the engine print tests. - [x] **215.17 Online return becomes a POS return.** *"same for return. If oms is getting return from shopify/website great"*. A return the channel sends is booked against its online POS bill automatically. Found: a Shopify refund already raised a return (99.6.2), always against the order's first bill; no marketplace connector sends returns, so those are taken at the till (215.14). Built: `salesInvoiceForOrderItems` picks the bill still holding every returned item (one bill per parcel), used by every return quoting an online order and by the Shopify refund, which also takes the goods into that bill's store; a repeated webhook finds its return by the stored key and replays it. Verified: `TestChannelReturnTakesTheRightBill` (second parcel's bill, HO, one return after a replay, listed in Sales History as Online), `TestStage999ShopifySync`, every engine test matching Return/Refund/SalesHistory. Guides: User Guide Shopify section, Shopify handbook. *Status: Done*

## POS — POS & Store Operations

### POS › Billing & till

- [x] **215.12 Sales History page.** *"There should an sales history page. To see sales and sales return."* One list of store and online bills and their returns, newest first, searched by any field (215.13); a row opens the bill with its three discounts, reprints it, or starts a return. Built: **POS → Sales History** (`SLH`, `public/view-sales-history.js`, `GET /api/v1/pos/sales-history`, `engines/pos_sales_history.go`): dates (last 7 days), Sale/Return, Store/Online, My stores/This store; totals line with item, offer and bill discount apart; 50 rows a page; the bill page has Return, Reprint, Open in Returns, Original bill. Verified: `TestSearchSalesHistory`; Guide `salehist` (totals match the API, a bill with all three discounts). User Guide §4.13, Admin Guide store access. *Status: Done*

- [x] **214.2 A till left on a completed sale takes no scans when opened again.** Found by the Guide `journey` rehearsal (ask 214): leave POS on a finished bill without pressing New Sale, open it later, and the scan box takes nothing and New Sale is not shown, so the cashier is stuck. Recommended: when POS opens on a completed cart, start a fresh sale (or show New Sale). The suite presses New Sale after each bill until this is fixed. Built (ask 220): POS opened on a completed sale starts a new sale (`renderPOSView`, `public/view-pos.js`). Checked: `node --check`. Not yet run live: the Guide POS suite still presses New Sale itself; drop that step and run it to verify. Verified (ask 221, 2026-11-16): the `journey` suite now leaves bill 1 without New Sale; Guide rehearsal on the working tree (reports/20261116-230618-journey) opened POS on a fresh sale with the scan box ready, and bill 2 went through. *Status: Done*
- [x] **214.5 Scanning the same barcode a second time adds 2 units.** Found by the Guide `journey` rehearsals (ask 214), 2 runs out of 2 (2026-11-16): the first scan of 0200000004798 adds 1, the second scan of the same barcode leaves 3 in the cart, so bill 2 sold 3 instead of 2. Single scans are right. Cause not found from the code: every add goes through one `existing.qty += 1` in `addResolvedItemToPOSCart`, so the second scan seems to run the add twice. The suite now records how many lookups each scan makes and takes the extra unit off with −. Recommended: reproduce by hand with a scanner; if it happens, guard the add so one Enter makes one add. Built (ask 220): one scan makes one add. A scan in flight blocks a second add of the same term and a stale search result is dropped (`posScanInFlight`, `posSearchGen`, `addPOSScanTerm`). Checked: `node --check`. Not yet run live with a scanner or the Guide suite. Verified (ask 221, 2026-11-16): same Guide `journey` rehearsal, bill 2 scanned the same barcode twice and sold exactly 2 units, no extra unit (it was 3 in 2 runs out of 2 before). A hand scanner check stays useful after deploy. *Status: Done*

### POS › Customer at the till & sale rules

- [x] **215.1 Name and mobile in two boxes.** *"Customer name and mobile number is diff section"*. The till's one box ("Mobile number (Enter), or a name") becomes Mobile and Name: Mobile looks the customer up, Name fills from it or names a new customer. Built: `#pos-customer-phone` + Name box; a new mobile opens the customer form with the mobile and the typed name. The app-wide phone hint sentence opts out with `data-phone-hint="off"` (the placeholder says it; it broke the till bar). Verified: Guide `posb2b` (boxes, new-customer form carries both). *Status: Done*
- [x] **215.2 B2B bill from the till.** *"GSt should be present. and If entered it will be b2b bill. ask few more mandatory fields ... like po number, date and address"*. A GSTIN box on the till; a valid GSTIN (checksum) makes the bill B2B and opens the buyer panel: legal name, billing address (state and PAN read from the GSTIN), buyer PO number and date, delivery (collected at store, or a ship-to address). A known GSTIN fills it from the Customer; a new one creates the Customer. Decided 2026-11-15 (ask-timeout took the recommended option): GSTIN, legal name, address line + city + PIN, PO number + date required (a setting can make PO optional), ship-to only when the goods are shipped; trade name, email and contact optional. Built: GSTIN box (Enter/change), checksum + state check, B2B panel (`view-pos-customer.js`), note `B2B · PO · shipped`, the GSTIN box fills from a customer's GSTIN; setting `pos.b2b.po_optional`. Verified: Guide `posb2b` - a wrong check digit is refused with no panel, blank Save names the missing fields, full Save quotes with the block. *Status: Done*
- [x] **215.3 B2B rules enforced and printed.** The server refuses a B2B bill missing a required field, not only the screen. Place of supply is the store's state when collected and the buyer's GSTIN state when shipped on the buyer's order (IGST Act s.10(1)(b) bill-to-ship-to; IGST when it differs). The tax invoice prints PO number and date, ship-to, place of supply and reverse charge No (CGST Rule 46). Decided: the server enforces the fields only when the till sends the `b2b` block; a bill whose customer merely has a GSTIN on file stays B2B the old way (no new refusal for existing flows). Codes POSOFF-0879 buyer, 0880 PO, 0881 ship-to; CUSTOM-0869 GSTIN. Verified: `TestPOSB2BBill`; Guide `posb2b` - Maharashtra store shipped to Karnataka gives IGST, saved bill has place of supply 29 and reverse charge No, the tax invoice data has B2B, GSTIN, PO, PO date, buyer and ship-to. *Status: Done*
- [x] **215.4 High-value rules asked before Complete Sale.** *"if it is 50K more ask pan and greater than 2L no cash pan mandatory etc. Read indian gst rule bring all the intricacy"*. Built in 85.C4: ₹50,000+ to an unregistered buyer needs name, address and state (Rule 46(e)); PAN above ₹2,00,000 or Form 97; cash of ₹2,00,000+ refused (s.269ST). Add: the till asks for each as the total crosses its line, and s.269ST counts the same customer's cash bills that day. Decided 2026-11-15 (ask-timeout took the recommended option): keep the law (address from ₹50,000, PAN above ₹2,00,000); the Settings value can lower PAN to ₹50,000 as a house rule. Built: the rule hint under the Total (*Cash over limit*, *Address needed*, *PAN or Form 97*, *B2B details needed*); s.269ST counts the same named customer's paid cash that day at any store (`CustomerCashToday`, walk-in excluded); a B2B bill needs no PAN. Verified: `TestPOSB2BBill`; Guide `posb2b` hints at ₹49,999 / ₹50,000 / ₹2,00,001 / cash ₹2,00,000, B2C and B2B, against the live policy. *Status: Done*

Ex- Marketplace may not sync sr."* A return of an online bill made at the till: credit note, stock back, refund mode, with no return from the channel. Bug found and fixed: Returns → Refund read only the first 200 refunds and said "No refund is pending" past that; it now asks for this return's refunds (`public/view-returns.js`). *Status: Done*

- [x] **214.3 Returns › Refund says "No refund is pending" once there are over 200 refunds.** Found by the Guide `journey` rehearsal (ask 214): `runReturnRefundFlow` (public/view-returns.js) loads `RefundRequest?limit=200` oldest first and looks for the return's refund in that page, so on a busy database the newest refund is never found and the return cannot be refunded on screen. Recommended: fetch by the field, `RefundRequest?return_request_id=<id>` (the doc list already accepts field filters). The suite flags this and finishes the refund by API. Fixed under 215.14: Returns → Refund asks for this return's own refunds (`public/view-returns.js`). Verified there by the Guide `salehist` run. *Status: Done*

- [x] **215.5 Three discounts kept apart.** *"There will be bill level discount and item level and offer level. All are three diff thing. Report should represent differently. Capture it diffferently."* Today the bill discount is folded into the offer share. Every line and bill stores item discount (the cashier's cut on a line), offer discount (offers only) and bill discount (the cashier's whole-bill % or ₹) apart; GST stays on the net. Order: item, then offers, then bill (as today). Built: the quote keeps item discount, offer discount and bill discount apart (each line's `bill_share`); the bill stores `item_discount` and `bill_discount`. The approval slab judges the line cuts and the bill discount together (`ManualDiscountTotalPct`), offers left out. Verified 2026-11-16: Guide posb2b (bar *Item* / *Bill*, saved bill, report rows). *Status: Done*

All std offer should be build and admin can choose the type of offer. It should be functional. Not failing."* Built: % off, flat off, Buy X Get Y free, any N for ₹P, gift with purchase, bonus points, over a bill, item, category, product group or SKU list. Add: Buy X get Y at % off; buy from list A get from list B; spend slabs; quantity slabs; fixed price; combo (one from each list for ₹P). Built (`engines/pos_offer_types.go`, migration `215_7`): all six, plus a *Design List* scope (every item of those designs, looked up for the cart's items only). Lists take SKUs, barcodes and design numbers. `TestPOSOfferTypes`, `TestValidateOfferTypes` pass. *Status: Done*

Built: days, hours (business time zone, over midnight allowed), an exclude list (even on a whole-bill offer), uses in total and per customer (Paid or Settled bills); **Stores** now limits every offer type, not only Bonus Points. Known limit: two tills taking the last use at the same moment can both get it. Tested in `TestPOSOfferTypes`. *Status: Done*
- [x] **215.9 Offer screen by type.** The admin picks the type and sees only its fields, with its Buy, Get or Combo item lists. Built: the form shows only the type's boxes (Combo hides Applies To), lists are text boxes, hours are time boxes; the save checks what the type needs and ignores other types' hidden boxes. Verified 2026-11-16: Guide `offers` suite, 22/22. *Status: Done*
- [x] **215.10 Bulk upload of offers.** *"All should be bulk uploaded."* One CSV of offers and one of their item lists; preview, then apply, with errors by row. Built: **Bulk Import → File**: *Offers* or *Item lists* (`offer, list, code`; Applies to / Get / Exclude / Combo N). Each named list is replaced; an offer with any bad row is left whole. ### POS › Store app (phone / tablet)

- [ ] **47.6 Real mobile/RF WMS/POS shell and physical reference journey** — closes A-06/A-39; not a desktop page squeezed into 390 px. **47.6.1-47.6.5 and 47.6.7 are built and green 2026-09-06.** The parent stays `[ ]` on **47.6.6 alone**, which needs physical hardware (device/scanner/printer certification and the gloves/weak-Wi-Fi/interruption run) and cannot be done from a development machine. *Status: Blocked - owner checklist §6 (only 47.6.6 is left: real devices, scanner and printer)*
  - [x] **47.6.1** `engines/field_semantics.go` — an explicit semantic registry (`text`, `phone`, `barcode`, `wave`, `lpn`, `lot`, `serial`, `number`, `date`) that **wins over** the substring inference, served to the browser by the existing `/api/v1/meta/field-formats` endpoint so the two sides cannot reach opposite answers. The A-06 bug reproduced and fixed: `mobile-pick-wave-id` contains "mobile", matched the phone tokens, and the phone keystroke filter stripped the letters out of a wave id **as the operator typed it**, silently, on a device where nobody could see the rewrite. **The more dangerous half was server-side** and is also fixed: `IsPhoneField` feeds `NormalizeDocumentPhones`, so without the override the server would have rewritten a stored wave id or lot number into E.164 on every save — mangling data at rest, not just while typing. The inference is deliberately **not** removed wholesale: its own comment explains it covers every phone field in the schema today and every one added later for free, and removing it would have silently stopped normalising all of them. `engines/field_semantics_test.go` asserts both directions — scan fields lose their filter, real phone fields keep theirs, `phone_country` stays excluded.
  - [x] **188.1** A-06's camelCase case was still red: `FieldSemantic` and app.js's `fieldSemantic` split words only on `-`/`_`/`.`, so `mobilePickWaveId` stayed a phone field (found running 47.0's red tests, 2026-11-14). Both now split camelCase words too (`splitCamelWords`), and `DetectFieldFormat`, app.js `detectFieldFormat` and `isPhoneFieldName` pass the raw name (they lower-cased it first, losing the word breaks); `customerMobile` stays a phone. `TestFieldSemanticsBeatSubstringInference` gained both cases; engines vet and the field-format tests pass, with the tagged `TestA06WaveIDFieldNotMisclassifiedAsPhone`; the browser mirror checked on a scratch port. *Status: Done*
  - [x] **47.6.2** The RF task shell (`renderRFTraceabilityView`, `public/app.js` + `.rf-*` in `styles.css`): no sidebar or top bar while a task is open (`body.rf-shell`, cleared in `renderViewContent` — the one place every view change passes through, so a bad exit cannot leave the app permanently chromeless), one instruction in words at the top, one scan target that keeps focus and re-takes it after every action, Enter advancing to the next field rather than submitting a half-filled form (a hardware scanner sends Enter after every barcode), a human label beside every code, no naked UUID, and 44px+ targets throughout. The supervisor path is a new **`FloorAssistRequest`** doctype the operator raises from their own session and a supervisor answers on their own device — explicitly **not** a Grievance (that is an HR complaint record; filing "I cannot scan this bin" into somebody's HR file is both wrong and quietly harmful) and explicitly **not** a password handoff, which is how shared logins start on a floor.
  - [x] **47.6.3** Built as **one responsive layer**, not six screen patches — every one of the six screens is assembled from the same vocabulary (`.table-panel`, `<table>`, `.form-input`, `.btn`), so fixing the vocabulary fixes all six *and* every screen added later; patching six screens would have fixed six screens. Three changes, all inside `@media (max-width: 820px)` so the desktop rendering is byte-identical: the sidebar goes **off-canvas** as a drawer (it was a hard 270px with no collapsed state anywhere in the stylesheet), `.table-panel` becomes a horizontal scroll container, and filter/action rows wrap. Plus a 400px reflow block for WCAG SC 1.4.10.
    **The measurement that matters, and a correction to how it was first taken:** `documentElement.scrollWidth` reported zero overflow *even before the fix*, because `.app-container` and `.main-content` are both `overflow: hidden` — so this application never scrolls sideways, it **clips**, which is worse and is exactly what "no *hidden* horizontal overflow" means. Re-measured against the right instrument (elements whose right edge passes a clipping ancestor that cannot be scrolled to reveal them), at 390×844 in Chromium:
    | | content width | sidebar cost | worst unreachable clip |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | before | 120px of 390 | 270px | POS 475px, Returns 553px — **7 of 7 screens** |
    | after | 390px | 0px | **0px on all 7** |
    Verified at 390 portrait, 844 landscape and the 320px reflow threshold, across POS, RF receiving, putaway, wave picking, sortation, loading dock, Returns and the RF shell, with zero console errors. The drawer was checked for keyboard operation too (toggle visible, `aria-expanded` tracking, scrim, Escape closes, focus returns).
  - [x] **47.6.4** The six-outcome vocabulary 47.6.4 names is complete and lives in one table (`RF_OUTCOMES`): success, duplicate, **wrong item**, **owner mismatch**, **hold**, offline — six because an operator must tell them apart *without reading*, and "already scanned" versus "the thing in your hand is not the thing on the screen" call for opposite next actions. Each carries a distinct tone, vibration rhythm, colour **and** border weight/style **and** text — four channels, because an aisle is loud (sound alone fails), bright (colour alone fails) and an operator may be colour-blind (colour alone fails again). Outcomes are resolved from the message-catalog **code**, not the HTTP status, since INVENT-0104/0106/0115 are all 422 and mean three different things on the floor — the first caller to actually use the precise coding `engines/traceability.go` has always returned.
    WCAG 2.2 AA work: a **global** `:focus-visible` ring (there were rings on ~8 components out of the whole application, so keyboard/scanner navigation was invisible everywhere else — SC 2.4.7), raised above sticky headers (SC 2.4.11, new in 2.2), and a 24×24 minimum target size on `.action-btn`/`.btn`/`.icon-btn` (SC 2.5.8, new in 2.2 — the AA threshold is 24×24, not 44×44, which is AAA).
    **Four real AA failures were found and fixed, by measurement rather than by eye:** `--warning-strong` on `--warning-soft-bg` was **2.86:1** in the light theme (fixed to 6.37:1); `--on-primary` on `--primary-color` was **3.90:1** in *both* dark blocks (fixed with the standard on-colour flip to dark-on-accent, 4.58:1, rather than darkening the accent — which would have cost it its contrast against `--panel-bg` and made primary buttons recede); six form controls had adjacent `<label>`s with **no `for=`**, so they were visible but silent to a screen reader; and two controls (`global-search`, `industry-selector`) had no accessible name at all.
    All of it is guarded by `internal/server/frontend_accessibility_test.go`, which parses `styles.css`/`index.html` and fails the build on regression — the same self-checking shape `route_capabilities_test.go`, `scope_policy_test.go` and the Stage 49 manifest test already use, because a one-time accessibility sweep lasts until the next screen. It computes real WCAG contrast ratios across **all three** token blocks (light, explicit dark, and the `prefers-color-scheme` default — a token correct in one and wrong in another is wrong for every user who never touched the theme toggle, which is most of them).
    **What this does NOT claim:** full AA conformance across all ~40 dynamically-rendered screens. That needs axe-core driving a browser over each one; what is guarded here is the foundation (tokens, focus, target size, reflow structure, shell labelling) plus the six screens measured above. The test says so in its own header rather than letting a green run imply certification.
  - [ ] **47.6.6** Device/scanner/printer certification and the gloves/weak-Wi-Fi/interruption/app-restart run. **[physical hardware/user input required — cannot be done from here]** *Status: Blocked - owner checklist §6 (needs real hardware)*
  - [x] **47.6.7** All four orphaned endpoints now have a caller, built into the task shell rather than as a sixth desktop WMS screen exactly as this item asked: `POST /api/v1/wms/batch/putaway` (bin-level lot assignment — what makes a lot visible to FEFO at all), `/batch/consume`, `/batch/expiry-sweep`, `/serial/status`. Live-verified against a scratch server on :8099: all four reachable and validating (422 on an empty body, 200 for the no-argument sweep), and the semantic registry confirmed served with `mobile-pick-wave-id → wave`.

  - **Four unrelated defects were found and fixed while doing this**, all recorded here so nobody re-derives them: (1) the RF shell CSS from 47.6.2 hid `.top-bar`, a class that does not exist in this markup - the header is `.app-header` - so the shell was still rendering the desktop search bar and industry selector above the task; (2) `db/migrations_stage47_2_*.sql` was not self-correcting on `POSPriceOverride.location` - an early draft declared it optional and `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` will not update an existing row, so any database that applied that draft would have read as unscoped for ever and leaked one store's override evidence to every other store; a follow-up `UPDATE` now makes the correction part of the migration. (3) `cmd/surfacescan`/`internal/securityscan` walked into `.claude/worktrees/agent-*` - full, **gitignored** copies of this repository that concurrent agent sessions leave on disk - and counted every `engines/*.go` outbound call site once per worktree, so `TestAttackSurfaceManifestIsCurrent` and `TestNoUnreviewedBypassPattern` failed for everyone the moment any session had a worktree open, for reasons unrelated to the code under review; `.claude` added to both scanners’ skip lists (`skippedDirs` in `bypass.go` already skipped `.git`/`node_modules`/`graphify-out`/`docs`, and `surface.go` repeated the same list inline, so both needed it). (4) `cmd/brainmap` emitted `BRAIN.md`'s lobe diagram in Go's randomised map order (sorted on weight alone, and `sort.Slice` is not stable), so `docs/brain/update-brain.ps1` failed at random on its own `-check` step - the redraw this repo's conventions require after any change that adds or moves a file. Fixed with the same (weight, from, to) tiebreak the region-edge sort in that file already used; verified deterministic over three paired runs.
- [ ] **102.1.2 Camera scanning** - the phone camera reads barcodes where the browser can (BarcodeDetector); a Bluetooth scanner works as a keyboard everywhere; typing the code always works. The camera is allowed for `/store/` only (Permissions-Policy), the rest of the ERP keeps it off. **Built 2026-10-19:** `permissionsPolicyFor` (`internal/server/middleware.go`) gives `/store` and `/store/*` `camera=(self)`, every other path `camera=()` (test `TestPermissionsPolicyCameraOnlyOnStoreApp`; checked live on both paths); full-screen camera with a frame, rear camera, Code 128 / EAN / UPC / Code 39 / ITF / QR / Data Matrix where supported, vibrate on a read, closes when the app is hidden. A scanner without Enter is caught as a burst of fast keystrokes (Stage 77's rule). **Not verified:** desktop Chrome on Windows has no BarcodeDetector, so the walk saw the "type or use a Bluetooth scanner" note instead of the camera - the camera path needs a real Android phone (102.5.2). **Limitation:** iPhone Safari has no BarcodeDetector either; reading barcodes there would need a JavaScript decoder library, ruled out by the no-dependency rule - iPhone users type or use a Bluetooth scanner (said in both guides). *Status: Blocked - owner checklist §6 (built, not verified)*
- [ ] **102.1.3 Push alerts** (Web Push) for approvals and low stock. **Built 2026-10-22:** `engines/web_push.go` - RFC 8291 aes128gcm + RFC 8292 VAPID with the stdlib (byte-for-byte RFC 8291 vector test); a key pair per client, private half under the new `WEB_PUSH_KEY` keyring; subscriptions per phone (`web_push_subscriptions`), sent only to the browsers' push services; a worker each minute diffs each subscriber's task-inbox approvals (every path into Pending Approval is covered) and the store's low stock (the Inventory Dashboard's rule, now one shared `inventoryLowStock`), pushes each thing once (`web_push_seen`; the first look only takes note), through the job runner (`web_push_delivery`; 404/410 end the subscription; nothing sent with side effects off or for a sandbox). App: Settings > Alerts; `sw.js` push + notification click. Verified: engine tests incl. delivery to a local push service that checks the VAPID signature and decrypts the message, and the worker (one push per new approval / low item, none on a repeat). Headless Chrome has no notification permission, so subscribing and showing an alert are the user's check (102.5.2). Found and fixed on the way: the Inventory Dashboard's low-stock list read Postgres integers as 0 and listed in-stock items as low. *Status: Blocked - owner checklist §6 (built, not verified on a phone)*
- [ ] **102.5.2 Real phones** - Android Chrome and iPhone Safari: install, scan, offline count, push alert; anything only the user can check on store hardware recorded as such. The user's checks: Android Chrome - install, camera scan (102.1.2), Settings > Turn on alerts, then an approval sent to you and an item sold out each show an alert that opens the right screen (102.1.3), a count in flight mode sent when back online; iPhone Safari - Add to Home Screen, alerts (iOS 16.4+), typing / Bluetooth scanner; a store PC with PrintBridge printing a Store Label Request from Print from Transaction (102.3.4); a basket number typed or scanned into the till's Find a basket (102.4.2). *Status: Blocked - owner checklist §6 (needs the user's phones)*

Recommended: convert JSON stamps to the business day (the 132.19 helper) in `catDay`, then re-run the registers that read event stamps. Found by the test-store flows on app (ask 230, owner "go"): bill BGTS2627000011 shows Document Date 2026-11-19 but is missing from GST Document Tax Detail and the Sales Register run for 2026-11-19 alone. `docRows` (214.4) dated `created_at AT TIME ZONE erp_business_tz()`: on a naive timestamp that turns the zone the wrong way, and production stores UTC, so the filter day ran 5h30 behind the row's own date (right on a dev machine, whose clock is IST). Gate entry's "arrived" time (`gate_entry.go`) has the same fault. Fixed in the one place every catalog document source runs through: `catCreatedDay` reads the stored clock first (`erp_local_date`), and gate entry does the same. Verified: new `TestCatCreatedDayOnAUTCClock` (a UTC and an IST session; the old expression put 01:30 and 09:00 IST on the day before), the business-day, gate-entry and control-total tests pass. Live check on app after the next deploy: the one-day GST report finds BGTS2627000011. *Status: Built, not verified*

### RPT › Report engine, filters & exports

- [ ] **26.10.6** [P2 — tier/scope decision] Dedicated data mart/read replica for heavy BI queries — only justified once real report-query load is measured against the live Postgres instance; don't build ahead of a measured bottleneck, per `CLAUDE.md`'s lightweight-first principle. **Go-ahead given 2026-07-27 covered all 5 P2 bundles, but this one item is deliberately NOT built as a result**: its own gating condition is "measure first," and there is still zero measured query-load evidence to build against — building a data mart anyway would be exactly the speculative-build this item was written to prevent, not a case of following a decision that's now been made. Built 26.10.7 instead (the measurement mechanism itself), which is what actually lets this item's own criterion ever be evaluated. *Status: Blocked - owner checklist §4 (built only when 26.10.7's measurement crosses the go_live_decisions.md §12 trigger)*
  - **Measured 2026-08-06, and the answer is still "not justified" — now on evidence rather than absence of evidence.** Re-greenlit by the user this date; measured before building, per the item's own condition. The live production database holds **no transactional data at all**: largest tenant table is `doctype_fields` (722 rows, metadata), `documents` 210, `audit_logs` 251, and `gl_postings`/`POSCart`/`SalesInvoice` are **empty**. Four user accounts. There is no query load, therefore no bottleneck, therefore nothing a read replica would relieve — it would add a replication topology, a second connection path and a staleness window to a system whose reports currently return instantly. Reported back to the user rather than building anyway.
  - **Concrete revisit thresholds recorded** (check via Reports → Report Performance, the 26.10.7 screen): any report's p95 duration over ~5s, **or** total report time over ~10% of DB busy time, **or** report queries measurably slowing transactional work (watch `db_pool.wait_duration_ms` on `/api/v1/health`). Written into `go_live_decisions.md` §12 so the condition is checkable by anyone, not just re-litigated.

Checked with node against 15 printer names: LD0801, Seznik, Godex and HP give PDF; Y468BT, TSC, TVS, Xprinter and Gprinter give TSPL; Zebra gives ZPL. `docs/guides/qz-printing-setup.md` now says how the pick is made and that PDF prints silently too. Only a PC's first pick is affected; existing Printer records are unchanged. Live check needs a release. **Ask 193:** printer-language routing verified locally. - [x] **203.3 New versions replace themselves.** Agent 0.3.0 adds `printbridge.update`: it reads `<page's own address>/downloads/printbridge-update.json` (version, size, sha256, Ed25519 signature by the release key built into the agent); when that version is newer it downloads the installer from the same address, checks size and sha256, waits for prints in progress, runs it silently and exits. The installer replaces the program in place and starts it (allowed addresses, logon start and printer choices are kept). The ERP asks once per page load. An allowed page cannot push an unsigned program. One installer file runs at most three times (a mislabelled release cannot loop), and `build.ps1` takes the version from the agent's `BuildVersion`. The signing key stays off the repo (`%USERPROFILE%\.printbridge\release-key.pem`); `build.ps1` signs; release gate G4d checks the update file against the installer. Agents before 0.3.0 cannot update themselves: Print setup offers the last download, and says so when an update fails. Verified: `go test` (update, agent); live on this laptop 0.3.0 → a 0.3.1 test build in 11 s through the real `client/printbridge.js` in Edge, then back to 0.3.0. Verified on app 0.25.0 (2026-11-15): the ERP's own `updateStatus()` returns `{"current":"0.3.0","latest":"0.3.0","status":"current"}`. *Status: Done*
- [x] **203.5 A computer keeps its printers after the browser is cleared.** The ERP asks PrintBridge for the computer's name (`networking.hostname`), which the agent never answered, so each browser made up its own (`PC-XXXX`). Agent 0.3.0 answers it; a browser that already has a name keeps it, so existing printer choices stay. Verified: the installed agent answers `networking.hostname` live. Verified on app 0.25.0 (2026-11-15): with nothing saved the browser takes the hostname (DESKTOP-HM7B3KD); a saved name (INSPECTOR-PC) is kept. *Status: Done*
- [ ] **203.6 Code-signing certificate, so Windows does not warn at the first download** [approved 2026-11-16, ask 218]. SmartScreen and Edge warn about an unsigned program from the internet ("Windows protected your PC" → More info → Run anyway), once per computer at the first install. Updates (203.3) are fetched by PrintBridge itself and are not warned about. Removing the first warning needs a yearly code-signing certificate (organisation-validated, cloud signing). Recommended: buy one before the first client outside the owner's shops installs. Built 2026-11-16: `installer/build.ps1` signs the agent and the installer before the update file records the installer's hash, from `%USERPROFILE%\.printbridge\codesign.json` (`thumbprint` = a certificate in the user's store, or `command` = the signer's own tool), with a time stamp; it stops when the certificate is missing, keyless or expired, or a signature or time stamp is missing. Checked with a throwaway self-signed certificate: both routes signed and time-stamped, `pbsign verify` still passed, a wrong thumbprint stopped the build, no settings file = unsigned as before. README *Code signing* says what to buy: an OV certificate with cloud or USB-token key, at most 460 days; Azure Artifact Signing does not take Indian organisations. [needs the owner: buy the certificate] *Status: Built, not verified - the warning goes once a bought certificate signs a release*

Target: the message within about 10 s, the wake still tried once, and a print still going out at once when PrintBridge comes up. **Built** (`public/qz-print.js`): the address that answered last is remembered and tried first; a round stops at 6 s; after a wake (10 s), a reinstall (25 s) or a recent wake (rest of 15 s) one socket a second polls that address instead of full rounds; soon after a failure one try stands for the round. Checked in Edge with a stand-in agent on spare ports (the real one untouched): down + wake fails 22.7 s then Retry 50.8 s before, **10.1 s then 5.0 s** now; woken 2.2 s; reinstalling 5.5 s; removed 0.1 s; agent on 8283 found in 15 ms and remembered. No fault: they are the Trusted Types trial (49.4.3) - the report-only policy reports every HTML sink, ~28 per page load by design, and the live check reloaded the page many times. - [x] **212.3 PrintBridge security review, and fixes.** A local service that takes print commands from a browser: check origin trust (lookalikes, `null`, http vs https, the *Allow printing?* list), signature checks on every privileged call, the self-update path (signature, size, hash, downgrade, where the file lands), request limits and what the log keeps. Fix what it finds; update PrintBridge's README where it is out of date. **Found and fixed (agent 0.3.2):** (1) any web page could read the PC's name (`websocket.getNetworkInfo`) and printer list (`printers.getStatus`) unsigned - now only a trusted page gets anything but `getVersion` (`authorize`, `trustedPage`); (2) a site could open boxes from many subdomains - now one *Allow printing?* box at a time and none after 3 answers of No until restart; (3) no socket cap - now 32, 503 past that. **Sound as found:** origin matching (whole-label wildcard, https only, `null` and IPs refused), signature on every privileged call, self-update (Ed25519 manifest from the page's own allowed site, newer-only, 64 MB cap, size + SHA-256 checked, per-user install so no elevation, 3-run limit), HTML render (temp dir, JavaScript off, 30 s), log keeps origins and errors, never print data. Tests `TestUnsignedCallsNeedTrustedPage`, `TestAskNoFlood`, `TestConnectionCap` + the agent package pass, vet clean; signed 0.3.2 built (`pbsign verify` ok) and copied into `public/downloads` (G4d checks hold). README and PROTOCOL.md updated (signature claim, untrusted pages, stale *Not done*); admin guide notes 0.3.2. Live: reaches PCs by self-update with the next release. Verified (ask 220): app.wholeops.in serves the signed `printbridge-update.json` for 0.3.2, identical to the local copy. This laptop's agent updated itself from 0.3.1 to 0.3.2 on 2026-11-15 at 22:40. *Status: Done*

- [ ] **213.1 Change the print for this one document before it prints.** User, 2026-11-15: *"If user want to change the paper. They can change easily for that txn before print. choose the template. They can just switch"*. Every normal print (POS bills and the other transactions) opens with its defaults filled in, and the user can switch the template, paper size and printer for this print only, see the preview and print. Design: the print dialog (`printTransactionDocument`, `public/trade-docs.js`) gains **Paper** (the sizes its templates come in, plus the built-in 80/58 mm roll where a bill offers it) and **Printer** (this computer's printers, preselected to the one for that paper; a different pick is for this print only, Change still sets the default). Choosing a paper picks its best template; choosing a template shows its paper. Built: Paper and Template selects with a printer row (`showPrinter`, `computerPrinters()` in `print-routing.js`); the pick goes to `printLayout(..., {printer})`. Verified locally in headless Edge on a scratch server: till bill 80 mm → A4 → 58 mm, sales invoice A4 → 80 mm, the printer row follows the paper (Receipt / Sales Invoice), preview redraws, no browser errors. Left: one real print to a picked printer on PrintBridge after the release. Ask 225: the till's Print Receipt and Reprint also switch (213.3, 213.4), and a print through PrintBridge reaches the printer again (225.1). Left: one real paper print, which needs the owner's OK and the owner at the printer, after the release that carries 225.1. *Status: Built, not verified*
- [x] **213.3 Choose at save as well as at print.** *"it can changed aswell while saviing or printing if rqd"*: the same choice is offered when a save prints, not only from the Print button. Design: no screen prints on save except the till, whose completed-sale panel appears the moment a sale is saved; its Print Receipt now opens the same dialog (preview, paper, template, printer) with the bill's defaults, instead of the roll-only preview. A sale with no tax invoice (not paid) prints the plain receipt as before. Built: `view-pos.js` Print Receipt calls `printTransactionDocument(..., {roll: true, cashTendered, onUnavailable})`; `printPOSBill` uses the same defaults and keeps the change line and reprint flag. Dialog checked as in 213.1; the till button itself not clicked (needs a finished sale). Verified (ask 225, 2026-11-16): Guide `tillprint` on the working tree (reports/20261116-233013-tillprint): a finished till sale's Print Receipt opened the dialog on the 80 mm roll with Roll bill, A4 redrew the preview with Standard layout, back to the roll, and Print sent one job to the stub printer. That run found 225.1. *Status: Done*
- [x] **213.4 Reprint can switch too.** *"In the reprint module as well. if they want to switch that time. It should be allowed"*. Design: Reprint already opens the print dialog, so it gets Paper and Printer too; a POS bill reprints on the roll by default. The content stays the frozen first copy; only the layout, paper and printer change. Built: `view-reprint.js` passes `roll` for a till bill; `IssuedPrint` keeps the frozen content but takes today's profile. Verified the Reprint dialog opens on the roll with the frozen copy (scratch server); no till bill in that print history to click from the screen. Verified (ask 225, 2026-11-16): same run, Print › Reprint listed the bill, its Reprint opened the dialog titled Reprint on the roll, and A4 and back redrew the preview. *Status: Done*
- [ ] **213.5 One switch maps the rest.** *"we will auto map to multiple print if required"*. Recommended reading: choosing a template picks its paper and the printer set up for that paper, and an operation set to print more than one document or copy (e.g. a B2B bill on A4 plus a thermal copy) prints each to its own printer. Building the first half with 213.1 (paper → printer). The second half (one Print sending two documents to two printers) is new scope. [needs design decision: user - confirm the second half is wanted] First half built in 213.1 (the printer follows the paper). **Approved 2026-11-16 (ask 224): the second half is wanted.** A Print Profile's *Extra prints* line (`Roll 80 x1, Tax Invoice A4 x2`; migration `213_5`); `printExtras` in `trade-docs.js` sends each extra to the printer for its paper and names any that fail. Guides updated. Stub-printer check passed (227.4: main + 2 extras = 3 jobs). On the stub each copy goes to its paper's printer (236.1: roll to the Receipt printer, A4 to the Sales Invoice printer). Needs a live run with a receipt and an A4 printer. *Status: Built, not verified*
- [x] **225.1 Silent bill prints fail with "The source image cannot be decoded".** Found by the Guide `tillprint` run (ask 225, 2026-11-16). Since 0.25.0 a comment in `trade-docs.css` holds the text "<style>", and `pageToCanvas` (`public/print-pdf.js`) put the CSS raw into an SVG image, so the image would not decode: every PrintBridge print of a till bill or trade document (PDF or ESC-POS) failed and the user fell back to the browser. Stickers use their own engine. Fixed once: `svgStyle()` in `print-pdf.js` escapes `<` and `&`; the dashboard PDF export (`view-dashboards.js`) had the same raw copy and uses it too. Release: new G8 check (CSS put raw into an SVG image in `public/*.js` stops the release), and Guide `smoke` (G10a) now runs `tillprint`. Verified: `tillprint` on the working tree sent 1 job to the stub printer (reports/20261116-233013-tillprint); `smoke` with the step passed it (reports/20261116-233343-smoke); the G8 check passes on the fix and stops on both old copies. Still broken on app and minn until the next release and deploy. *Status: Done*

- [ ] **49.2 Identity, authentication, recovery and machine identity** — prevent account takeover without making safe recovery impossible. — *2026-09-08: 49.2.2 closed. 2026-09-09: 49.2.4 closed (see its own note). Parent stays open pending 49.2.1, 49.2.3, 49.2.5-49.2.8.* *Status: Blocked - only 49.2.3's real-key check, owner checklist §10*
  - [x] **49.2.1 Identity lifecycle.** Unique human identity; no shared cashier/admin/support account; joiner/mover/leaver approval; immediate disable; session/service-token revocation; dormant-account expiry; authoritative HR/admin linkage and periodic access recertification. **Built 2026-10-27 (next 20, erp-d3):** sign-ins are recorded when a full session is issued (password / MFA / store PIN, never purpose tokens), best effort; re-enabling gives a fresh window. India-corporate `Word@123` patterns) is the one choke point `handleChangePassword`, `CompletePasswordReset` and `handleCreateUser` all call now — length (new `security.password_min_length` setting, default 12, Stage 30.7 registry pattern), denylist match, username-equality and a cheap ascending/descending/all-same-character sequence check. **Real pre-existing bug found and fixed**: `handleLogin`'s bcrypt check had a plaintext fallback (`u.PasswordHash != req.Password`) left over from old local-seed configs — every real seed is already a bcrypt hash so it had no legitimate caller, but its failure mode was backwards for a security control (a `password_hash` value that was ever plaintext, from a bad migration or manual SQL fix, would authenticate via a non-constant-time `!=`); removed, bcrypt is now the only accepted form. Added bcrypt cost-migration (`handleLogin` re-hashes at `bcrypt.DefaultCost` on successful login if the stored hash's cost is lower — best-effort, does not bump credential_version since it's not a real credential change). The MFA recovery check (`handlers_mfa_recovery.go` 41) and the store PIN checks use 401 the same way. Recommended: answer a wrong current password or PIN with 403 and its own catalog code, keeping 401 for a refused session only. Not built: outside ask 206. Built (ask 220): a wrong current password (Change Password, recovery email, MFA recovery) answers 403 `GLOBAL-0312` "Your current password is not right.", and the user stays signed in. Store PIN *sign-in* keeps 401: it has no session to drop, and the PIN-set screens already use 403. User SOP updated. Verified: `TestWrongCurrentPasswordKeepsTheUserSignedIn` plus the server tests. The map was bootstrapped mechanically (moduleGate name, or `/admin/<segment>`, or the first `/api/v1/` path segment) rather than hand-typed per route — 454 routes made individual review impractical, a systematic rule is more auditable than 454 separate judgment calls; every `/api/v1/admin/*` route became `LevelAdmin` (Super-Admin-only), which changes no legitimate behavior since routes.go's own repeated "HR/Admin-only, enforced in each handler" comments already claimed this was already true — now actually enforced in one place. **Real bug found and fixed while wiring this in**: the first working version used `http.DefaultServeMux.Handler(r)` to recover the matched pattern, which is empty in every unit test (`Run()` — the only code that registers routes.go onto `DefaultServeMux` — is never called by a test, it blocks on `ListenAndServe`), so every handler-level test started failing 403 across the board. Fixed with a dedicated `capabilityLookupMux` built once from `routeCapabilities`' own keys (see that var's comment) — works identically whether a real server or a direct `apiMiddleware(handler)` test call. **Second, pre-existing bug found (not fixed, out of scope for this item)**: `document_numbering_api_test.go`'s update call built `/api/v1/doc/RFQ/`+firstID with the server-issued number's own `/`-separated form (`engines/numbering.go`'s default separator) left un-encoded — Go's real mux only matches `{id}` against an *escaped* slash (verified empirically: a raw `/` in the path does not match `/api/v1/doc/{doctype}/{id}` at all, an encoded `%2F` does). Fixed the test (`url.PathEscape(firstID)`, matching what a real client must send). **This same gap likely affects production today**, independent of this session's work: `public/app.js:18858`'s real edit-save call (`` `/api/v1/doc/${currentDoctype}/${editingDocID}` ``) does not encode the id either, and every numbered doctype (`engines/document_numbering.go`'s 11-doctype list — PurchaseOrder, GRN, ASN, RFQ, VendorQuote, TransferOrder, ExpenseClaim, Leave, EmployeeLoan, Grievance, ProductionOrder, Attendance) gets a `/`-separated series number by default — so editing one of these via the path-addressed route may already 404 silently, unrelated to authorization. Not investigated further or fixed here (found via an isolated Go mux experiment, not confirmed against the live app) — **[needs a follow-up session to confirm live and, if real, fix `editingDocID` encoding in `app.js` or move that save path onto the existing body-upsert form `PrepareDocumentNumber`'s own comment describes]**. `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` all green; the stage47redteam suite's A-01 test (previously the only reproducible authorization finding) now **passes** — Cashier gets 403/`GLOBAL-0011` from trial balance, audit log and system log, exactly the required closure — while A-02 through A-06 remain red/unaddressed, unaffected by this item.

  - [x] **47.1.4 Scope semantics.** Enforce entity/location/owner/self scope separately from role capability. A `NULL` location/owner is not global visibility unless the role has an explicit global capability. Add cross-entity/location/owner and employee-self negative tests. **Built 2026-09-05:** `engines/scope_policy.go` + wiring in `handlers_core_doc_engine.go`. The old single inline clause (`AND (COALESCE(data->>'location', data->>'location_code') = $n OR … IS NULL)`, applied to every doctype for every non-Super-Admin) conflated three different situations; the registry now separates them, keyed off each doctype's own `mandatory` flag in its `doctype_fields` seed: **(1)** no location field at all → unscoped, visible (MarketplaceSettlement/LogisticsBooking — the original comment was right); **(2)** location declared *optional* (19 doctypes: Printer, Zone, ShiftAssignment, TaskCompletionLog…) → an attribute, not a scope, so a missing value stays visible but a present one is still filtered; **(3)** location declared *mandatory* (26 doctypes: POSSession, GRN, PurchaseOrder, Wave, WarehouseTask…) → a missing value is an anomaly and is now **denied**, which is the item's "a NULL location is not global visibility" case. Two dimensions the old clause had no notion of were added: **self** (11 `employee_id` doctypes — a Cashier/Store Manager holding only *read* is confined to its own employee record; a role holding create/update on a doctype it administers for the team is exempt, listed explicitly in `selfScopeExemptions` from the shipped seed's own grants) and **owner** (4 mandatory-`owner_id` 3PL doctypes, gated on a new `scope.owner.global` capability; per-session owner scope itself is 47.5's work, so the capability is granted to Store Supervisor/Administrator today and 47.5 replaces the default rather than inventing the mechanism). Fails **closed**: a session whose scope value cannot be resolved (no linked `Employee` record, no owner) matches no row rather than every row. **A real client-side-filtering leak found and closed while doing this**: `renderMyRequestsTab` (`public/app.js:14700-14707`) fetches the *whole* of `/api/v1/doc/Leave`, `/ExpenseClaim` and `/Grievance` and filters by `l.employee_id === employee.code` **in the browser** — every colleague's leave reasons, expense amounts and grievance descriptions were already on the wire; server-side self scope closes it with the page rendering exactly the same rows. **Legal entity is deliberately NOT a dimension yet, on measured evidence**: `Location.legal_entity` is optional and populated on 1 of 112 rows, `JournalVoucher.entity` optional and populated on **0 of 199**, and `StatusTransitionRule.entity` — the only mandatory "entity" field in the schema — does not mean a legal entity at all (its own Select options are `Order,OrderLine,FulfillmentOrder,Shipment`; stored values are doctype names like `ExpenseClaim`), so scoping on it would be wrong, not merely strict. `ScopeDimension` names it so 47.12.2/47.12.3 extend this file. A doctype the registry does not know (one built at runtime through the Doctype Builder) keeps exactly the pre-47.1.4 clause. **Cost, per 47.18's ledger rule**: the only new per-request work is the self dimension's Employee lookup, so it is resolved lazily — on GET only, and only for a doctype that declares a self field (a write to Payslip/Leave pays nothing) — and `db/migrations_stage47_1_role_templates.sql` adds the partial index `documents((data->>'user_id')) WHERE doctype='Employee'` that this lookup shape (shared with the pre-existing `GET /api/v1/hr/my-employee`) never had. Location and owner add no query at all: they are extra `AND` predicates on the list query that was already running. 8 tests in `engines/scope_policy_test.go`, including a completeness test that re-parses `db/*.sql` and fails the build if the registry drifts from the seeds.

### SEC › Security hardening & findings

- [ ] **26.1.3b** Activate the Cloudflare edge WAF — managed rules, L7 DDoS absorption and bot controls. The domain and zone now exist (`wholeops.in`, 2026-08-14), so the external prerequisite is met; both records are still grey-cloud. *Status: Blocked - owner checklist §1 (waits on the owner's Cloudflare account; both records are still grey-cloud)*
  - **⚠️ Correction (2026-08-15): "pure activation" was wrong, and the reason is worth keeping.** The plan of record was orange-cloud + CIDRs in two places + lock the origin. That combination **breaks Let's Encrypt renewal on this box**: once the origin firewall admits only Cloudflare, ACME cannot reach it — TLS-ALPN-01 cannot work through a proxy that terminates TLS, and HTTP-01 through the Cloudflare proxy is documented as unreliable. Nothing would have failed on the day; `app.wholeops.in`'s certificate would simply have stopped renewing and expired on **2026-11-12**, with the per-tenant on-demand path breaking sooner. DNS-01 is the usual escape and is refused here for the reason 26.1.3a already gave: it needs a provider plugin, therefore `xcaddy`, therefore a custom Caddy binary to keep patched.
  - **Resolution (user decision, 2026-08-15): a Cloudflare Origin CA certificate** — free, 15-year, wildcard, issued by Cloudflare and trusted only by Cloudflare, which is exactly the CF↔origin hop. No ACME, no plugin, no renewal, and it is what makes a fully locked-down origin possible rather than merely aspirational.
  - **Repository work for this is DONE (2026-08-15) and was not previously counted:** `deploy/enable_tls.sh` gained a `--cloudflare --origin-cert --origin-key` mode and `deploy/Caddyfile` was parameterised so **one** file describes both postures instead of two files drifting. `--cloudflare` fetches Cloudflare's published CIDRs at install time (never hardcoded — a stale list silently mis-attributes client IPs), **fails closed if the fetch fails** rather than installing a partial trust list, writes them into *both* Caddy's `trusted_proxies` (with `trusted_proxies_strict`) *and* `TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS` in `/etc/erp/erp.env`, and restarts the app. One command owns both halves because their drift is silent: an unlisted edge node is mistaken for the client and every visitor collapses into one rate-limit bucket. It also **inverts the DNS pre-flight check** instead of skipping it — in `--cloudflare` mode the name must resolve *into* a Cloudflare range, because installing an Origin CA certificate while the record is still grey-cloud serves browsers a certificate only Cloudflare trusts and takes the site down.
  - **Still open — the parts that are genuinely portal-side:** orange-cloud both records, SSL/TLS mode → Full (strict), generate the Origin CA certificate, enable the free managed WAF ruleset + Bot Fight Mode + Authenticated Origin Pulls, a rate-limiting rule on `/api/v1/login`, a cache-bypass rule (Cloudflare caches `.js`/`.css` by default and `public/app.js` has no content hash in its name, so a deploy would otherwise serve stale JS), and restricting inbound 80/443 in the **DigitalOcean cloud firewall** — not just `ufw`, which was never what was blocking traffic here (44.9).

  - [x] **47.8.2** Replace inline handlers with event delegation/module listeners and adopt nonce/hash CSP without `script-src 'unsafe-inline'`; enforce in report-only mode first, then blocking. — *2026-10-03: strict same-origin candidate now reports violations to a bounded same-origin handler; targeted Log Hub, report, generic-record and asset row actions moved to listeners/data attributes. More than 90 handler locations remain; enforcing CSP stays unsafe.* — *2026-10-04: **DONE.** All **126** inline event-handler attributes removed — 109 across `app.js` and the 18 view modules, 17 in `index.html` — replaced by one document-level delegated dispatcher (`handleDelegatedAction`) driven by `data-act`/`data-act-args` that `actionAttrs()` emits. Converted mechanically after classifying every handler first: 107 of 109 were a single function call, and the two that were not became named functions (`goToDefaultView`, `selectFieldText`) because the dispatcher calls one function per action and has no statement sequencing — sequencing would mean evaluating source, which is the thing being removed. **This was a security fix, not only a CSP enabler**: an inline handler interpolated its arguments as JavaScript source inside an HTML attribute, so a record id, SKU or doctype containing a quote closed the attribute and the string and executed whatever followed — a live injection sink wherever that value came from imported data, a connector payload, a report row or a CSV cell. Arguments now travel JSON-encoded and HTML-escaped, so a hostile value can only arrive as data. **The new harness found a latent primitive in the dispatcher itself**: `window['eval']` is a function, so a hostile `data-act` name with a string argument would have been arbitrary code execution. `resolveActionFunction` now requires a plain identifier that is an **own** property of `window` (excluding `constructor`/`__proto__`/`toString`) and is not a native built-in — an allowlist by construction rather than a denylist chasing the platform. Not reachable today (every name is a string literal in this repository), so defence in depth rather than a patched hole. **CSP flipped report-only -> blocking**, both phases measured in real Chromium across all 29 dispatched views: the strict candidate reported **zero** violations of any directive *before* it was enforced (that is what authorised the flip), and after enforcing `script-src 'self'` — zero enforced violations, zero `script-src` violations, zero page errors, re-confirmed a third time after the dispatcher hardening. **No nonce and no hash list, deliberately**: there are no inline `<script>` blocks for a nonce to permit (all six scripts are external files), a nonce would add a per-request value that defeats static caching of `index.html`, and `script-src` nonces do not whitelist handler *attributes* anyway — that needs `'unsafe-hashes'`, a weaker position than removing them. The report-only header now carries the next candidate, strict `style-src`, whose 629 inline-style violations are visible but not enforced, so the same two-phase mechanism is in place for that step. All **128** `data-act` reference sites were also checked statically against every way this codebase publishes a function to `window`; all resolve except four pre-existing dangling names in dead `index.html` modal markup (reported, not fixed — removing those overlays is a UI decision). `TestSecurityHeadersEnforcesStrictScriptSrc` now guards the inverse of what it used to: that the exception is not quietly reintroduced. See `project_ledger.md` §190.*

Effective logout, per-session revocation and the server idle limit are built (162.15 step 1). Left: short-lived access and rotation (ADR step 2); cookies only if a customer requires them.   - [x] **49.4.4 Shared request envelope.** Allowlisted method/content type/encoding; bounded headers/body/multipart/decompression/JSON depth/array/string/numeric range; strict duplicate/ambiguous parameter handling; deadlines/cancellation; safe error catalog and correlation ID with no stack/SQL/path/secret leak.
    *Built 2026-10-09 (five-item pass, item 3), to the scope the user set.*
    - **Where:** new `internal/server/request_envelope.go`, called once from `apiMiddleware`
      and from `publicAPIMiddleware` (the second caller boundary). It replaces the bare 2 MB
      `MaxBytesReader`.
    - **Limits** (in ADMIN_GUIDE §D's table): methods allowlisted (405 `GLOBAL-0303`). JSON or
      multipart only, plus per-route CSV/CSP types, and compressed bodies refused (415
      `GLOBAL-0304`). Bodies 2 MB, or 10/16/100/5 MB on the upload and import routes (413
      `GLOBAL-0305`). Headers 100 fields/32 KB (431 `GLOBAL-0306`). JSON is streamed and checked
      for depth 32, lists of 50,000, strings of 1 MB (2 MB for StickerTemplate), exact duplicate
      keys including escape-spelled ones, and trailing values (400 `GLOBAL-0307`). A 55 s
      deadline flows through `r.Context()`; an expired one answers 504 `GLOBAL-0308` instead of
      `writeEngineError`'s empty 499.
    - **Measured first:** stored documents reach depth 3, arrays of 7 and strings of 654. A
      sticker logo is ≤400 KB, about 546 KB as a data URL, and all of a design's logos share the
      one `design` string. `max_import_rows` is 20,000. Measuring found that the old 2 MB cap
      silently overrode every larger per-handler limit, because a nested `MaxBytesReader` cannot
      raise an outer one: media upload, imports and the hook were all really capped at 2 MB.
    - **Decisions:** case-variant keys are allowed, because `copies_override`, `dispositions` and
      `exchange_for` are SKU/line-keyed maps. A body with no `Content-Type` is read as JSON and
      fully bounded; the allowlist's real job is blocking preflight-free `text/plain`/form
      bodies. Rejections are not logged (log-flood risk).
    - **Tests:** `request_envelope_test.go` has one case per limit through `apiMiddleware`
      (status, code, matching correlation id, no leak). Large legitimate payloads pass: a
      3.0 MB 20,000-row multipart CSV, a 1.6 MB three-logo sticker design and a 2,000-line PO;
      the first two are refused on a default route. Plus deadline-vs-abandoned.
    - **Live on scratch :8147:**
      - a real three-logo StickerTemplate save returned 200;
      - a real 3.4 MB/20,000-row import preview passed the envelope;
      - dup key → 400, TRACE → 405, text/plain → 415, each with its correlation id;
      - a Playwright sweep of all 53 lazy views + 4 setup screens made 341 API calls with zero
        envelope rejections.
    - **Not done (follow-up 49.4.4a):** numeric range bounds and duplicate query-parameter
      handling, which the user's scope did not include.

      but `tenant_default` it was ~17 ms/row, about 20 round trips per Item row. `GetSettingInt`
      ran once per non-empty field, each re-resolving the tenant schema. On top of that, every row
      re-read the doctype meta, field permissions and the Master flag, did an EXISTS, then an
      upsert. **Fix, in the shared core only** (`runDocDataImportContext`/`importBatch`, so plain
      CSV, PIM preview and templated import all get it): `importLookups` resolves meta, the
      length setting, permissions and the Master flag once per file. Each batch prefetches its Link
      targets with one `id = ANY` query per Link field (self-links stay per row) and writes its
      valid rows in one `unnest` upsert whose `RETURNING (xmax = 0)` gives the create/update
      split. `ValidateDocument` became a wrapper over `validateDocumentWith`, and now reads
      `field_max_length` once per call rather than once per field, which also helps every
      single-record save.
      **Found on the way and fixed in the same statement:** the import upsert had the
      cross-record-type overwrite `7657453` fixed for generic create. An Item row whose code was a
      Brand's id overwrote the Brand's data and reported "created". The import now refuses that
      row. **Measured, same 20,000-row fixture, scratch tenant:** preview 6 m 0.6 s → **5.2 s**;
      import → **4.4 s**. **Live** on scratch :8147 (tenant_default): 20,000-row
      `POST /pim/import/Item/preview` **200 in 6.9 s**, 0 rows written. **Tests:**
      `engines/import_perf_test.go`, both A/B-confirmed to fail on HEAD:
      `TestItemImportPreviewFitsTheDeadlineAtTheRowCap` (must stay under 27.5 s, half the
      deadline) and `TestBatchedImportKeepsPerRowAnswers` (1,203 rows over 3 batches: Link
      found/missing/Cancelled, file-order ids, re-preview = all updates with no write,
      cross-type refusal, restricted fields).
      **Still per row:** a row with no id/code draws its sequence number with one query. Every
      Master import (Item, Vendor, Customer…) carries a code, so it isn't affected. Guides:
      USER_GUIDE §8d.5 large-file note, ADMIN_GUIDE §D.

### PLAT › Errors & messages

- [x] **206.1 Sign-in and session refusals stay out of System Errors.** Every 401 answer (session expired, wrong password, failed MFA) was written to `system_error_logs` - about 8 "Session expired" rows each time a session ran out (one per call the page made, sign-out included). The sign-in module already counts, locks and audits sign-ins (`LOGIN`, `ACCOUNT_LOCKED`, `LOGIN_ABUSE`). Built in the one shared logger, `logForEntry`. Shipped in 0.26.0 (release gates passed, ledger §393); ticked under ask 220. *Status: Done*
- [x] **206.2 System Errors lists real faults, grouped.** `/api/v1/logs/system` keeps only faults, using the filter the Control Panel's Errors (24h) and the error-rate alert already use (Stage 104.2): 5xx answers, panics, background-job and browser failures. Refusals a user can correct stay in the log for the correlation trail. Identical rows become one row with a count, first and last seen. Shipped in 0.26.0 (ledger §393); ticked under ask 220. *Status: Done*
- [x] **206.3 Resolve and Copy for fix.** User chose "Fix list" (2026-11-15). The fault detail has **Resolve**, which hides that fault until it happens again, and then it comes back by itself. It also has **Copy for fix**, a ready brief to paste to Claude. Code fixes still ship through a release; the app never changes its own code (Stage 14.13). Shipped in 0.26.0 (ledger §393); ticked under ask 220. *Status: Done*
- [x] **206.4 Real faults reach the fix queue.** Patch intake read only severities `ERROR`/`PANIC`, but a 5xx answer is logged with its catalog severity (`GLOBAL-0302` is `Critical`), so no server fault ever reached `patch_proposals`. It now uses the same fault filter as 206.2. Shipped in 0.26.0 (ledger §393); ticked under ask 220. *Status: Done*

### PLAT › Database, migrations & data integrity

- [ ] **51.1a** Decide and run a production data backfill for Master records already stranded with `id != code` (created before 51.1 shipped). Needs a read-only audit query against prod first (how many rows, which doctypes) before deciding an UPDATE approach — do not run against production without explicit sign-off given it mutates live primary-key-equivalent values. **[needs design decision: user — backfill approach and timing, likely alongside the batched deploy]** — *2026-10-04: **the audit half is done; the decision is still the open part.** Decision taken this pass (user): write the read-only audit query only, no UPDATE authored yet. Delivered `scripts/audit_master_id_code_drift.sql`, six sections: (1) stranded rows per doctype with date range, (2) stranded/healthy/no-code totals so the ratio is visible, (3) a **collision check that must be empty before any backfill** — rows whose wanted id is already occupied by a different record, i.e. two records claiming one identity, which no plain UPDATE can resolve, (4) a **blast-radius** count of transactions already referencing each stray id, since an UPDATE that moves a Master id without rewriting those trades one broken reference for another on documents that currently work, (5) a 50-row sample to eyeball, (6) a per-tenant headline so it is clear whether `minn` is the only affected tenant. Deliberately placed in `scripts/`, **not** `db/` — `db/migrate.go:361` executes every `.sql` file in that directory, so this must never sit there. Pass the schema with `-v schema=...` (defaults to `tenant_minn`); the in-file default is `\if :{?schema}`-guarded so the command-line override actually wins. **Validated end-to-end against the local dev DB**: ran clean and found 12 stranded rows of 503 Master rows in `tenant_default` (Item ×4, plus Brand/CartonType/Channel/DockDoor/HoldCode/ReasonCode/Trailer/Zone), **zero collisions**, and 10 transactions already pointing at stray ids (9 for Item, 1 for ReasonCode). Nothing was written and **no production connection was made from here** — running it against prod, and the backfill decision that follows, are still the user's. Section 6 was first written as a `DO` block using `RAISE NOTICE`; that lands on stderr and interleaved unpredictably with the result sets when redirected to a file, so it was rebuilt with `\gexec`. Note the dev-DB numbers are not a prediction of prod's — prod has months of real master data created before 51.1 shipped. Full detail `project_ledger.md` §184.* **Recommended (ask 175):** the owner runs `scripts/audit_master_id_code_drift.sql` read-only on prod and shares the output; a backfill is written only if it shows stranded rows and the collision check is empty. *Status: Needs decision* **Owner, 2026-11-14 (ask 178): all recommended.** *Status: Blocked - owner checklist §4 (the owner runs `scripts/audit_master_id_code_drift.sql` read-only on prod)*
  — *2026-10-06, read-only prod audit (user-approved; session forced default_transaction_read_only):* production was redeployed 2026-10-05 23:52 UTC with Stage 51-53 (not Stage 57). **tenant_minn has 3 Master rows and all 3 are stranded** (id = random UUID, code = series): Vendor Siyona (Vendor/HQ/2026/000001), Location BLR Warehouse (Location/HQ/2026/000001), Item Zircon Earring (Item/HQ/2026/000001) - these are exactly the user's three reports (vendor not suggesting in PO; the TO "Location/HQ/2026/000001 is not a registered Location" error; GRN showing no item). Collision check empty; blast radius 1 document referencing the stray Item id. tenant_default: 11 of 250 stranded. Backfill is a contained UPDATE plus one reference rewrite - **Done for tenant_minn 2026-10-06/07 with the user's explicit go-ahead:** fresh encrypted backup `custom_erp_20261006T034651Z.dump.enc`, then one guarded transaction (no FKs or uuid columns reference documents; each statement had to change exactly one row and no target id could exist) moved the 3 masters to id = code and re-keyed the earring's PIMProductProfile (`Item/HQ/2026/000001::profile`, product_id updated). Audit re-run: tenant_minn 0 stranded / 3 healthy. **tenant_default (11 of 252) left untouched - not approved.** Stage 57's ResolveLocationReference and name-display lookups already tolerate stranded rows in the UI; Link validation (by id) still needs the backfill.

Manufacturing's MRP tab and its Quality tab's delegation into the shared, still-core `renderDocTableView`); a simulated chunk-load failure on a fresh page (`route.abort`) shows the retry panel with zero uncaught page errors, and a subsequent real load succeeds. **Measured result**: cold-core dropped 347,586 → 337,701 bytes (~9.9 KB, ~2.8%) and initial JS 307,602 → 297,717 bytes across these 4 of ~56 total dispatched views — a small but real, fully mechanical, fully verified dent; closing the budget gap needs this same proven pattern applied to most of the remaining ~52 views, which is real, scoped, repeatable follow-up (same shape as the `guardAgainstDoubleSubmit` rollout's own 3-session mechanical scale-out), not a one-session undertaking. `node --check`/`go build`/`go vet` clean; one full `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` run 100% green (after two `attack_surface.json` regenerations for the new static files — `go run ./cmd/surfacescan -out %TEMP%\...` then copied in via PowerShell, no permission block this time); `graphify update .` and `pwsh docs/brain/update-brain.ps1` run after (the latter needed one `brain.map.json` edit: added a `public/view-*.js` glob to the existing "SPA Shell" region so future chunk files auto-claim without a further edit).
