### UI › Accessibility & theming

- [ ] **50.13 UI and accessibility acceptance.** BLD-033–040 plus 50.8's shared label/focus repairs: task-led start pages, dense tables, consistent forms, truthful transaction states, recoverable interruptions, visual hierarchy, keyboard/screen-reader/contrast/locale acceptance, physical RF/printing and observed user tasks. Extend existing 47.6/47.10/47.12/47.14/47.15 and Stage 31 work. — *2026-09-24: BLD-033 (task-oriented role navigation and onboarding) built and verified — new `home` view/`DEFAULT_VIEW`, a static task-tile list gated through the exact same permission/module predicates the sidebar already uses (no role-name branching), an approvals-pending stat, a "Recent" panel (new opt-in `sort=recent` on the generic doc-list endpoint, caught and fixed a real query-param collision bug via its own new test), and scope-led "Get started" setup guidance reusing Stage 41's existing hint machinery. Deliberately excludes every Settings/admin screen from the tile grid — the retired 2026-08-01 Dashboard's own failure mode. Live-verified with real Playwright across all 5 named personas (cashier, Accounts Payable "buyer", Picker "floor operator", Accountant, tenant admin) plus Store Supervisor, each reaching its main task in one click; Picker (most permission-limited) shows zero unauthorized/disabled tiles; a freshly-provisioned empty single-package tenant confirmed real module-gating and the setup-guidance/no-Recent states. `USER_GUIDE.md`/`USER_SOP.md`/`UAT_CHECKLIST.md`/KB updated (all previously said Reports is the landing screen). BLD-034–040 remain open. Full detail `project_ledger.md` §166, build checklist BLD-033 entry.* — *2026-09-25: BLD-038 (accessibility and localization acceptance) built and verified. Found and fixed a real, app-wide gap: every `.modal-overlay` dialog hides via `opacity:0`, not `display:none`, so a closed dialog stayed fully reachable by Tab and exposed to the accessibility tree — a 120-press Tab walk from Home hit 53 invisible controls belonging to closed dialogs. Fixed via synchronous `inert` toggling at each real open/close pair (a first MutationObserver-based attempt was live-tested and found to race a synchronous `.focus()` call in `openFieldModal`, so it was replaced, not patched over). Also found and fixed: screen-reader announcements were almost entirely absent app-wide (one `aria-live` region existed in the whole app) — `showToast`/`renderPageBanner`/the shared custom-dialog markup now announce; Home's new approvals stat-card (plus two pre-existing tiles of the same shape — exec-dashboard, OMS) was a plain unreachable `<div>` with a click handler, fixed via a new shared `makeClickable()` helper; the new `.stat-card[role="button"]`/`.home-recent-item` focus rings were invisible under Windows High Contrast (`forced-colors`) since `box-shadow` is not repainted there — fixed by pairing them with a real `outline`. Contrast (text/non-text, both themes), non-color status meaning, target size, 320px reflow, text-spacing (1.4.12) and reduced-motion all came back clean on live measurement. Locale/RTL: no i18n/RTL infrastructure exists in this app (by design, not an oversight) — scoped smoke tests only, no unsupported language coverage claimed. BLD-034–037/039/040 remain open. Full detail `project_ledger.md` §168, build checklist BLD-038 entry.* — *2026-09-27: BLD-037 (coherent visual hierarchy and support context) built and verified. Four real findings, all fixed at a shared choke point: the environment/support banner truncated to one ellipsized line with the rest reachable only via hover, silently hiding 3 of its 4 real operational notices on any touch device — fixed with a real "Show more"/"Show less" toggle and JS-computed body padding; Home's lone pending-approvals stat-card stretched to the full shared-grid row width and read as a half-empty panel — capped via a scoped `.home-stats-row` override (same precedent as `.oms-tile`); the backend's own promised "correlation_id shown to the user" was never actually read off the error envelope by the frontend — now surfaced on the modal and Page banner (152/302 catalog rows) error paths; every generic doctype-table screen's raw PascalCase identifier ("PurchaseOrder", "SalesInvoice") and a stray placeholder subtitle ("Pluggable module metadata records database") leaked to real users app-wide (~90 Setup-sidebar entries, verified live with zero misfires) — fixed via a new `humanizeIdentifier()`/`getDoctypeLabel()` pair applied only at doctype-heading call sites, deliberately not at `getTranslatedLabel` itself (a first attempt there was live-caught corrupting unrelated text like "3PL"→"3 PL" and reverted before shipping). Incidental bug found and fixed along the way: a bogus, wrongly-cased "← Back to home" breadcrumb on every cold doctype-table deep-link load. `go build`/`go vet` clean; one full `go test ./... -p 1` run clean except the pre-existing, unrelated Stage 51.8 "Stores" module gap. BLD-036/039/040 remain open. Full detail `project_ledger.md` §169, build checklist BLD-037 entry.* — *2026-09-28: BLD-034 (usable dense tables) built and verified — the generic doctype-table screen used to fetch the server's already-capped 500-row default once and paginate/search that fixed batch client-side, so on BLD-043's seeded 250k-row tables every row past the first 500 was permanently unreachable and search could never see past that same window. Pagination/search/counts are now real server round trips (SQL-side search, opt-in `count=true` total via a new `X-Total-Count` header so the endpoint's other ~13 callers, Link-field typeaheads chief among them, don't pay for it); found and closed a field-permission search-oracle gap in the same pass, and found and fixed a genuinely pre-existing bug while verifying "persistent context" — a doctype-table refresh had never actually restored its page/search since Stage 41 (indistinguishable from a fresh deep link), now fixed narrowly so hint/bookmark links still land clean. Explicitly measured and flagged, not fixed: free-text search is correct at scale now but slow (~2s at 250k rows — the per-row JSON scan itself, not the query shape) — a real fix needs a trigram/GIN index, a separate decision. Live-verified with Playwright against the 250k-row seeded dataset; three fresh full-suite rounds green. Full detail `project_ledger.md` §172, build checklist BLD-034 entry.* — *2026-09-29: BLD-035 (consistent forms and truthful transaction outcomes) built and verified. The generic doc engine's create/update response hardcoded `{"status":"saved"}` regardless of the document's real resulting status — fixed to report the value actually persisted (including a re-approval-on-edit reset to Pending Approval); `handleDecideApproval` similarly only ever echoed the bare decision even though approving a VendorInvoice override finalizes it straight to Paid — added the row's real post-decision `document_status`. Wired five previously-silent call sites (generic form save, PO/generic/QualityInspection submit-for-approval, decideApproval, savePurchaseOrder's plain create/edit path) to show the real outcome via one new shared helper. Added missing `Date`/`Currency` fieldtype rendering to the generic form (dozens of real Date fields across many doctypes were falling into a plain text box with no date picker). **Found and fixed live, not originally scoped**: `editDocRecord`/`handleDynamicFormSubmit`/`deleteDocRecord` built their edit/delete URL with the document id un-encoded — since Stage 51.1 made `id` equal the human-readable `code` (e.g. `"Vendor/HQ/2026/000002"`), this 404'd Edit-load, Save and Delete for the great majority of real records; fixed to match the PO composer's own already-correct `encodeURIComponent` pattern. Live-verified end-to-end with real Playwright against a disposable Postgres/scratch-server instance; three fresh full-suite rounds green. Full detail `project_ledger.md` §175, build checklist BLD-035 entry.* — *2026-09-30: BLD-036 (recoverable empty/loading/error/offline states) worked — real progress, stays open. `apiFetch`/`apiUpload` gained a client-side timeout (30s/90s, honest wording that never claims a mutating call's server-side effect is known once the client gives up waiting); a new shared `guardAgainstDoubleSubmit` helper closes a real duplicate-document-creation gap on the generic record form and the PO composer (each POST gets a fresh server-numbered code, so a double-click could create two real documents); `renderApprovalsView`'s load failure now uses the existing `renderErrorPanel` retry instead of a dead-end panel or blank screen. Live-verified with real Playwright: exactly one POST fires for a synchronous double-submit on both forms; a normal save still completes correctly; the real 30s timeout, a hard connection failure, a real 409 conflict (server's actual message surfaces, edit preserved) and a native-validation block on an empty mandatory field all behave correctly with input never lost. One full `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` run 100% green (no Go files touched) — notably the two Stage 51.8 "Stores" tests now pass on this tree, worth a follow-up check. **Stays open**: the double-submit guard covers two save paths, not every create/decide action app-wide; only 1 of ~54 "Failed to load" call sites got the retry treatment this pass; "stable loading layout" wasn't assessed. Full detail `project_ledger.md` §176, build checklist BLD-036 entry.* — *2026-09-30 (continued): worked all three named gaps in the same thread. Double-submit guard extended to `decideApproval`'s Approve/Reject buttons and the GRN workbench's "Post Receipt" (both newly guarded); POS checkout's manual disable was converted to the shared helper too, with its internal price-changed recursive retry repointed at the new unguarded inner function so re-entering the guard mid-retry doesn't no-op it. 13 more of the 54 "Failed to load" dead ends fixed on the five personas' critical paths (Fulfillment, Yard Board, WMS Cockpit, Chart of Accounts, Trial Balance, Purchase Orders list, OMS Orders, System Status, all four Log Hub tabs) — ~34 of 54 now have a working retry. "Stable loading layout" actually assessed: `renderView`'s scratch-buffer/atomic-swap navigation and the generic doctype-table's stale-while-revalidate pagination (BLD-034) are both already correct; several bespoke section-refresh functions (yard board, cockpit, OMS orders, similar WMS/Finance screens) still blank-then-fill instead, named as real follow-up. **Two real bugs found by live-verifying these fixes, not by reading them**: the Chart of Accounts retry closed over `renderView`'s off-screen scratch-buffer container (the exact bug class `renderView`'s own comment warns about) and silently rendered into a detached, removed node on retry - fixed by resolving the live parent at click time instead; the Log Hub jobs-pane retry left `jobsLoadFailed` (a `const`) stale after a successful reload, so the old failure message never cleared - fixed by making it `let` and reassigning it in `reloadJobs()`. Live-verified with real Playwright against the same disposable Postgres/`:8096` scratch server: busy-label + exactly-one-POST confirmed for `decideApproval`/`createGRN` under a forced race; POS checkout's guard/validation-restore mechanics confirmed (full price-change retry path not re-driven live, verified instead by `node --check`/full `go test`/manual review since the change was a mechanical extraction); 6 distinct retry surfaces confirmed via forced-500-then-succeed round trips, including the two bugs above confirmed fixed after the event. One full `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` run 100% green (no Go files touched). **Stays open, narrower now**: ~30 other bespoke create/save/submit composers (HR, Assets, Manufacturing, other WMS) remain unguarded; ~20 of the 54 dead-end sites are action-triggered fetches on otherwise-interactive screens, assessed but not individually re-verified; the named bespoke section-refresh screens still need the stale-while-revalidate retrofit. Left 1 real GRN + 1 rejected stale Item approval in `custom_erp_test` from verification (not rolled back - harmless in a disposable fixture). Live-verified exactly-one-POST-on-double-click across 4 representative modules (RFQ/BOM/Asset/YardCheckIn); full `go test` regression green. Found and named, not fixed (decision-sized, broad blast radius): a real timezone-skew bug in `AuditVerificationOverdue`/`audit_checkpoints.created_at` (server session timezone leaking into a naive `TIMESTAMP` column, misread as UTC by Go) — a third instance of a bug class already hit twice before. ## OPS — Release, Hosting & Operations

### OPS › Release pipeline & gates

- [ ] **26.11.6** Hypercare window definition (on-call owner, duration, rollback trigger) for the first pilot. **[needs user sign-off + 20.1/20.2]** — **plan drafted 2026-08-06**: `docs/operations/hypercare_plan.md`, with a stated default for each of the four decisions (14 days; named primary + secondary; business hours +2h rather than 24/7, since 24/7 for one maintainer degrades daytime response rather than adding cover; and pre-agreed rollback triggers). Reuses `incident_runbook.md`'s existing P0/P1/P2 severities and rollback mechanics rather than restating them. The rollback triggers are the substance: any P0 unfixed within 2h, two P1s in 24h, **any doubt about data integrity** (GL not balancing, stock not reconciling, an approval that appears to have bypassed maker-checker — roll back first and investigate from backup, because a corrupted ledger worsens with every transaction written on top), or two consecutive failed nightly backups. **Explicitly records its own blockers**: 20.1 (escalation contacts) and 20.2 (alert webhook) are hard preconditions — without them an out-of-hours P0 reaches nobody, which makes every response target in the plan fiction. 26.11.3's drill is also marked a precondition rather than a parallel task. *Status: Blocked - owner checklist §6 (waits on 26.2.2 and the owner's sign-off)*

Preserve open blockers and unsupported configurations; backlog completion alone never establishes Production/Certified status. *Status: Blocked - owner checklist §6 (waits on the owner's approvals and a named pilot)*

Local commits of only the working records (and the D6 code map) no longer stop a deploy. Runs for real at the next deploy. *Status: Blocked - owner checklist §10 (built, not verified)* **Ask 232 (2026-11-17, 0.27.0 = 584eebd on app + minn):** -Check reported the run under test; the wait path (-Yes while GitHub runs) was not exercised - the deploy started after the run was green. *Status: Built, not verified*
- [ ] **177.5 The click's own gates must take about a minute.** In the 0.24.0 run, G7 (graphify, KB, checklist map, brain, docs, doclint) took 8m14s, G4b (`go run ./cmd/releasever`) 89s and G2 67s. Each needs to be measured and cut, for example with prebuilt tools and work only on files that changed, until the click itself runs in about a minute. **Built (2026-11-14):** G1 always uses `%TEMP%\erp_release` (Go's build cache is keyed by folder). G2 asks git about 200 paths per call. G4b's rule moved to `internal/releasever`, so `cmd/releasever` builds without engines. G5 runs only when the catalog's inputs changed. update-docs' tree check reads file times, not contents. G7 no longer rebuilds the code graph: it files every new file and draws the map from the last graph. After the push, `Invoke-BrainRedraw.ps1` rebuilds the graph and map in the background at below-normal priority (gate G13b), and Deploy D6 copies the map into the tree for the records commit. `release/scripts/graphify_update.py` caches graphify's path lookups: 466 s to 130 s, and the graph is identical node for node and link for link. Verified: all four scripts parse; a real background redraw took 198 s (2 workers, coverage 100%, worktree removed, graph kept); D6 copied the redrawn commit's map, only noted another commit, and left changed files alone; a dead redraw reads as failed. The click's total time is measured on the next release. *Status: Blocked - owner checklist §10 (built, not verified)* **Ask 232 (2026-11-17, 0.27.0 = 584eebd on app + minn):** Measured in the real click: 2m57s to push (G4c 60 s, G7 74 s, G10b 8 s); the target is about a minute. **Ask 235 (2026-11-17):** G4c's stripped Linux build now runs in the background at below-normal priority, starting right after G7's KB step (the last step that rewrites a file the server embeds), and is judged with G4f just before G7's document register (which records the ledger's hash). The cold core is still judged at once in G4c, and a stop kills the build. G7 is now timed in parts (KB; checklist map and brain; register) so the next click shows what to cut next. Verified: the script parses; the same Start-Process call, run as `go build -n`, exits 0 with Linux as the target and `-s -w` link flags, and the click's own GOOS stays empty afterwards. *Status: Built, not verified - the next real click measures it*

It now keeps drawn counters (`GREATEST`). TestStage95SignatureAgreesWithCurl read the fake S3's map without its lock, which `-race` flags (`fakeS3.object`). When `public/` changes, G4 bumps `app.js?v=`, `styles.css?v=`, `qz-print.js?v=` and `VIEW_MODULE_VERSION`, but not `index.html`'s `theme-boot.js?v=`, so a changed theme-boot.js can be served from a browser's cache. - [ ] **56.2 SB-015 — encrypted database link.** `engines/security_baseline.go:283` blocks a
  production boot when `DATABASE_URL` names a non-loopback host with an `sslmode` that permits
  cleartext. On the droplet Postgres is on loopback so the rule sleeps; on Railway the database is
  a separate service, so it fires correctly. Add `sslmode=require` and confirm the negotiated
  connection with `SELECT ssl FROM pg_stat_ssl WHERE pid = pg_backend_pid();` — the setting being
  present is not evidence it was honoured. Prefer `verify-full` + `sslrootcert=` if Railway
  publishes a CA for its Postgres; record which mode was actually achieved and why. *Status: Blocked - owner checklist §1 (part of 134.11's Railway staging, which waits on the user's Railway token)*
- [ ] **56.3 SB-012 — proxy trust against Railway's edge.** `engines/security_baseline.go:251`
  blocks production when `TRUST_PROXY` is on and `TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS` is empty, because
  `clientIP()` (`internal/server/middleware.go:606`) would then accept any caller's own
  `X-Forwarded-For` — forging the identity that login rate limiting, lockout and the audit trail
  all record. Railway does not publish its edge proxy's address range, so this is **determined
  empirically, not from the code**: with 56.1 still in warn mode, log `r.RemoteAddr` on one real
  inbound request, then pin that range (expect a private IPv6 or CGNAT block) into
  `TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS`. Verify by sending a request with a forged `X-Forwarded-For` and
  confirming the resolved IP is the socket peer, not the header. Record the range and the date —
  if Railway renumbers, this silently degrades to trusting a header, so it needs a re-check item
  at cutover and a note in the runbook. *Status: Blocked - owner checklist §1 (part of 134.11's Railway staging, which waits on the user's Railway token)*

> - **49.5.6a Offline sales are validated before they post.** User: *"There will be a validation of offline sales screen. Once its validated it will deduct. Validation of erp barcode vs scanned. Else won't be posted."* A sale sent with `offline_synced` no longer posts stock / GL at checkout: it waits on an Offline sales validation screen, each line's barcode scanned at the till against the item's ERP barcode; only an all-match sale can be validated (then it posts and deducts), anything else is never posted. **Built:** held in `pos_offline_sales`, decided on POS → Offline Sales (capability `pos.offline_validate`: Admin, Store Supervisor), POSOFF-1361 / 1362; Validate replays the held sale through checkout as the original cashier in the original session. `TestStage4956a*` (7); live check on a scratch server. *Status: Done - released 0.19.0 `efc6403` (user in erp-cd: "Push all and deploy all"), deploy pending.*
> - **49.5.6b Vendor state is mandatory; no PO without it.** User: *"This will be avoided by making vendor state mandatory to save and not allow po if state is absent."* A supplier can't be saved without a state; a PO for a supplier with no state is refused; the PO's interstate flag comes from vendor state vs the receiving location's state, so the free `interstate_override` no longer decides it. **Built:** `Vendor.state` always mandatory (a GSTIN fills it, a contradicting one is refused); PURCHA-4961 on save, submit, import, bulk upload, RFQ award and supply proposal; supply type always derived, the override ignored, the PO screen ticks removed. `TestStage4956b*`. *Status: Done - released 0.19.0 `efc6403` (user in erp-cd: "Push all and deploy all"), deploy pending.*
> - **49.5.6c BOM variance acknowledgement only through its own action** (not answered; standard practice taken): `bom_variance_acknowledged` can't be set or cleared by an ordinary Production Order save; a dedicated acknowledge action needs the manufacturing permission and a reason and writes an audit row. **Built:** a save / import can't set or clear it (MFG-4962); the existing acknowledge action needs ProductionOrder update and a reason (MFG-4963 / 4964) and audits the quantities before and after. `TestStage4956c*`. *Status: Done - released 0.19.0 `efc6403` (user in erp-cd: "Push all and deploy all"), deploy pending.*
> - **49.3.2a Unknown client code is refused.** User: *"Fix it."* An unregistered client id at sign-in (or on any pre-sign-in request) is refused instead of landing on the default client. **Built:** `db.GetTenantSchema` answers `ErrUnknownTenant`; a no-session request naming an unregistered client gets SAAS-0188. `TestStage4932a*` (2). *Status: Done - released 0.19.0 `efc6403` (user in erp-cd: "Push all and deploy all"), deploy pending.*
> - **49.2.1a A store switch counts as activity.** User: *"Yes, take std practice."* Any fresh session (sign-in, store switch) stamps the account's last activity, so the dormant sweep never disables someone who is working. **Built:** a store switch and an own password change stamp `last_sign_in_at`; a support session does not. `TestStage4921aStoreSwitchCountsAsActivity`. *Status: Done - released 0.19.0 `efc6403` (user in erp-cd: "Push all and deploy all"), deploy pending.*

Nothing should be booked twice."* A booking whose request may have reached the courier keeps its claim; a retry checks with the courier first (or waits for a person to confirm) instead of sending a second booking. **Built:** claim and reference saved before the call; an uncertain booking keeps its claim; a retry asks the courier first (Delhivery, Shiprocket) or is refused with INTEGR-4718 until **Resolve** on OMS → Ship; one AWB per courier / one live AWB per parcel (migration `47_11_8a`). `courier_idempotent_47118a_test.go`, `TestCourierResolveBooking47118a`; live check. > - **49.5.6d Offline sales follow-ups** (found building 49.5.6a). [needs decision, recommended answers: (1) at the till, an exact SKU scan of an item with no barcode counts as a scan, or its offline sales can never be validated - yes; (2) expected cash at till close includes held offline sales, as its own line - yes; (3) a validated sale posts on the till's sale date, or the validation date when that period is locked - yes]. Limits: a held bill loses its scans on resume; CSV-uploaded lines count as not scanned. User, 2026-10-29: *"all recommended"* - all three yes. **Built:** (1) the till's exact match (`posExactBarcode`) takes the SKU of an item with no barcode, so it adds without the list and counts as scanned; (2) cash of Pending offline sales is its own line `held_offline` in the X-report and the till close, and in expected cash (`sessionHeldOfflineCash`); (3) a validated sale's GL rows, stock ledger lines and bill take the till's sale time (`dateOfflineSaleTx`, as a backdated JV, 73.D.16), or today when that day is in a Closed period; the audit row says which. Limits: bill number from today's series; a sale sent on to Approvals is dated on approval; gift-card / loyalty rows keep the validation date.
